Or even just a random thought about something I forgot for the league. And then... I realize I just missed the last few minutes of what they were saying.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been having these problems with attention and distractibility? Have they been going on since childhood, or is this something that started more recently?\n\nPatient: No, not childhood. It's... more recent. Probably about a year and eleven months. \n\nThings just shifted around then. I had to take on a lot more of the league and... outreach responsibilities because... well, a friend of mine passed away. So, it's just been since then, really.\n\nDoctor: Since your friend passed away about two years ago, have you been feeling persistently sad or down most days, or have you lost interest in things you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: No... not really. I mean, it was hard at first, obviously. But I don't feel sad all the time. I still like doing the things I usually do. It's just... the work stuff that's hard to get through.\n\nDoctor: When you're trying to focus on work, do you find yourself feeling restless or fidgety, like you need to get up and move around?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I sit at my desk fine. I don't feel like I have to get up and pace or anything. It's just... hard to keep my mind on the screen.\n\nDoctor: Besides work and meetings, do you notice these attention problems in other areas of your life—like when you're at home, spending time with friends, or doing recreational activities?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I don't... I don't think so. When I'm at home or just doing my own thing, it's fine. It's mostly just the work stuff that's the issue.\n\nDoctor: When you're doing something you find interesting or enjoyable—like managing the league activities you mentioned—do you have the same trouble keeping your attention on that, or is it easier to focus on things like that?\n\nPatient: No, the league stuff is fine. I can... I can focus on that okay. It's just the work things.\n\nDoctor: When you're doing other administrative or boring tasks outside of work—like paying bills, doing taxes, organizing things at home—do you have the same trouble focusing that you experience with work reports?\n\nPatient: I don't know... I guess my wife usually handles most of the bills and that kind of stuff. So I don't really have to do it much.\n\nDoctor: When you're doing something at home that requires you to sit and concentrate for a while—like reading instructions for something, or working through a problem that needs focus—do you have the same difficulty keeping your attention, or is it easier?\n\nPatient: I don't... I'm not really sure. I don't usually sit down and read manuals or... things like that. If something needs putting together, I just kind of figure it out as I go. So, I haven't really noticed.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"
  },

    "role": "user",
    "content": "Return the JSON now."
  }

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I'm here because... well, I manage a few health clinics, and lately, anything that requires me to just sit down and focus... like monthly reports or planning big events across the sites... it just feels like I hit a wall. I keep putting it off, even though I know it needs to get done.\n\nDoctor: When you sit down to work on these reports or planning tasks, do you find it hard to concentrate and keep your mind on what you're doing, or is it more that you feel unmotivated or anxious about starting them?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure. I haven't... I don't really analyze it that much. I just know when I sit down at my desk, it doesn't happen. I guess it just feels hard to do.\n\nDoctor: When you try to sit down and work on these reports, does your mind tend to wander to other things, or do you find yourself getting distracted easily?\n\nPatient: I guess so. It happens in meetings, too. I'll be... trying to listen to a budget update or something. But if a phone vibrates, or... people are talking out in the hall, it pulls me away. Or even just a random thought about something I forgot for the league. And then... I realize I just missed the last few minutes of what they were saying.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been having these problems with attention and distractibility? Have they been going on since childhood, or is this something that started more recently?\n\nPatient: No, not childhood. It's... more recent. Probably about a year and eleven months. \n\nThings just shifted around then. I had to take on a lot more of the league and... outreach responsibilities because... well, a friend of mine passed away. So, it's just been since then, really.\n\nDoctor: Since your friend passed away about two years ago, have you been feeling persistently sad or down most days, or have you lost interest in things you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: No... not really. I mean, it was hard at first, obviously. But I don't feel sad all the time. I still like doing the things I usually do. It's just... the work stuff that's hard to get through.\n\nDoctor: When you're trying to focus on work, do you find yourself feeling restless or fidgety, like you need to get up and move around?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I sit at my desk fine. I don't feel like I have to get up and pace or anything. It's just... hard to keep my mind on the screen.\n\nDoctor: Besides work and meetings, do you notice these attention problems in other areas of your life—like when you're at home, spending time with friends, or doing recreational activities?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I don't... I don't think so. When I'm at home or just doing my own thing, it's fine. It's mostly just the work stuff that's the issue.\n\nDoctor: When you're doing something you find interesting or enjoyable—like managing the league activities you mentioned—do you have the same trouble keeping your attention on that, or is it easier to focus on things like that?\n\nPatient: No, the league stuff is fine. I can... I can focus on that okay. It's just the work things.\n\nDoctor: When you're doing other administrative or boring tasks outside of work—like paying bills, doing taxes, organizing things at home—do you have the same trouble focusing that you experience with work reports?\n\nPatient: I don't know... I guess my wife usually handles most of the bills and that kind of stuff. So I don't really have to do it much.\n\nDoctor: When you're doing something at home that requires you to sit and concentrate for a while—like reading instructions for something, or working through a problem that needs focus—do you have the same difficulty keeping your attention, or is it easier?\n\nPatient: I don't... I'm not really sure. I don't usually sit down and read manuals or... things like that. If something needs putting together, I just kind of figure it out as I go. So, I haven't really noticed.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned the league activities are fine and you can focus on those. When you're doing something at work that's interesting to you or that you enjoy—not just the boring reports—do you still have the same trouble concentrating, or is it easier to focus on work tasks that you find more engaging?\n\nPatient: I guess... if it's something I actually like doing at work, it's a little easier. But honestly, most of it is just the reports and the meetings. So... I don't know, I haven't really thought about it.\n\nDoctor: When you have to do something that's not very interesting but still needs to get done—like sitting through a long meeting you don't particularly care about, or doing a task that feels tedious—do you have the same trouble keeping your attention focused?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess so. If a phone vibrates somewhere, or I hear people out in the hall, I just... lose track. Or sometimes I just start thinking about something else, like stuff I need to do for the league, and then I realize I haven't heard anything they said for the last few minutes.\n\nDoctor: On days when you don't have as much work to do, or when your workload feels lighter, do you still have trouble focusing on work tasks, or does it get better when there's less pressure on you?\n\nPatient: I don't really know. I haven't really noticed a difference. Work is just... work.
